\displaystyle\quad-\lambda^{2}\left(\mathfrak{F}_{L_{0}L_{0}}\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)+F^{(2,1)}_{L_{0}L_{0}}\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)+F^{(2,2)}_{L_{0}L_{0}}\cos\left(\frac{2u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)+\mathfrak{h}_{L_{0}L_{0}}\right)+\lambda^{3}(g^{-1})^{(\geq 3)}(\partial u_{0})^{2}

\displaystyle=-\lambda^{2}\left(\mathfrak{F}_{L_{0}L_{0}}\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)+F^{(2,1)}_{L_{0}L_{0}}\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)+F^{(2,2)}_{L_{0}L_{0}}\cos\left(\frac{2u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)+\mathfrak{h}_{L_{0}L_{0}}\right)+\lambda^{3}(g^{-1})^{(\geq 3)}(\partial u_{0})^{2},

(A.2)

where we also used (
2.4
). The absence of a
\lambda^{0}
term in (
A.2
) explains why there is no
\lambda^{-1}
term in the expansion of
\tilde{\Box}_{g}g_{\alpha\beta}
given by (
4.10
). Similarly we have

\displaystyle g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}u_{0}\partial_{\nu}f

\displaystyle=-L_{0}f+\lambda^{2}(g^{-1})^{(\geq 2)}\partial u_{0}\partial f,

(A.3)

and

\displaystyle\tilde{\Box}_{g}f

\displaystyle=\tilde{\Box}_{g_{0}}f+\lambda(g^{-1})^{(\geq 1)}\partial^{2}f,

(A.4)

for
f
a scalar function. Note that (
A.4
) also gives the expansion of the wave operator applied to the background term in (
4.1
):

\displaystyle\tilde{\Box}_{g}(g_{0})_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\Box}_{g_{0}}(g_{0})_{\alpha\beta}+\lambda(g^{-1})^{(\geq 1)}\partial^{2}g_{0}.

We now apply (
3.9
) to each oscillating term in (
4.1
), using systematically (
A.2
), (
A.3
) and (
A.4
). For
\lambda g^{(1)}
this gives

\displaystyle\tilde{\Box}_{g}\left(\lambda\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)F^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\lambda}\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)g^{-1}(\mathrm{d}u_{0},\mathrm{d}u_{0})F^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}-\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\left(2g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}u_{0}\partial_{\nu}F^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}+(\tilde{\Box}_{g}u_{0})F^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\tilde{\Box}_{g}F^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=-\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\left(-2L_{0}F^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}+(\tilde{\Box}_{g_{0}}u_{0})F^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\left(\mathfrak{F}_{L_{0}L_{0}}\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)+F^{(2,1)}_{L_{0}L_{0}}\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)+F^{(2,2)}_{L_{0}L_{0}}\cos\left(\frac{2u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)+\mathfrak{h}_{L_{0}L_{0}}\right)F^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda\left((g^{-1})^{(\geq 1)}F^{(1)}\partial^{2}u_{0}+g^{-1}\partial^{2}F^{(1)}\right)+\lambda^{2}\left((g^{-1})^{(\geq 2)}\partial u_{0}\partial F^{(1)}+(g^{-1})^{(\geq 3)}(\partial u_{0})^{2}F^{(1)}\right).

For
\lambda^{2}g^{(2)}
this gives

\displaystyle\tilde{\Box}_{g}\left(\lambda^{2}\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha\beta}\right)

\displaystyle=-\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)g^{-1}(\mathrm{d}u_{0},\mathrm{d}u_{0})\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha\beta}+\lambda\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\left(2g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}u_{0}\partial_{\nu}\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha\beta}+(\tilde{\Box}_{g}u_{0})\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda^{2}\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\tilde{\Box}_{g}\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=\lambda\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\left(-2L_{0}\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha\beta}+(\tilde{\Box}_{g_{0}}u_{0})\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha\beta}\right)+\lambda^{2}\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\tilde{\Box}_{g}\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda^{2}\left((g^{-1})^{(\geq 2)}(\partial u_{0})^{2}\mathfrak{F}+(g^{-1})^{(\geq 1)}\partial^{2}u_{0}\mathfrak{F}\right)+\lambda^{3}(g^{-1})^{(\geq 2)}\partial u_{0}\partial\mathfrak{F},

and

\displaystyle\tilde{\Box}_{g}\left(\lambda^{2}\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)F^{(2,1)}_{\alpha\beta}\right)

\displaystyle=\lambda\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\left(-2L_{0}F^{(2,1)}_{\alpha\beta}+(\tilde{\Box}_{g_{0}}u_{0})F^{(2,1)}_{\alpha\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda^{2}\left((g^{-1})^{(\geq 2)}(\partial u_{0})^{2}F^{(2,1)}+(g^{-1})^{(\geq 1)}\partial^{2}u_{0}F^{(2,1)}+g^{-1}\partial^{2}F^{(2,1)}\right)+\lambda^{3}(g^{-1})^{(\geq 2)}\partial u_{0}\partial F^{(2,1)},

and

\displaystyle\tilde{\Box}_{g}\left(\lambda^{2}\cos\left(\frac{2u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)F^{(2,2)}_{\alpha\beta}\right)

\displaystyle=-2\lambda\sin\left(\frac{2u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\left(-2L_{0}F^{(2,2)}_{\alpha\beta}+(\tilde{\Box}_{g_{0}}u_{0})F^{(2,2)}_{\alpha\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda^{2}\left((g^{-1})^{(\geq 2)}(\partial u_{0})^{2}F^{(2,2)}+(g^{-1})^{(\geq 1)}\partial^{2}u_{0}F^{(2,2)}+g^{-1}\partial^{2}F^{(2,2)}\right)+\lambda^{3}(g^{-1})^{(\geq 2)}\partial u_{0}\partial F^{(2,2)}.

Collecting terms for each power of
\lambda
in the previous expressions, we obtain the various expressions of Proposition
4.1
.

A.2
Proof of Proposition
4.2

The quadratic non-linearity
P(g)(\partial g,\partial g)
is of the form
(g^{-1}\partial g)^{2}
, see (
3.2
) for an exact expression. Therefore, we have schematically

\displaystyle P^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{(g_{0}^{-1}\partial g_{0})^{2}}_{\vcentcolon=A}+\underbrace{g_{0}^{-2}\partial g_{0}\partial u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}}_{\vcentcolon=B}+\underbrace{(g_{0}^{-1}\partial u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)})^{2}}_{\vcentcolon=C},

since
(\partial g)^{(0)}=\partial g_{0}+\partial u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}
. Note that the purely background term
A_{\alpha\beta}
simply corresponds to
P_{\alpha\beta}(g_{0})(\partial g_{0},\partial g_{0})
. For
B_{\alpha\beta}
, we have from (
3.2
)

\displaystyle B_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{(\alpha}(g_{0})_{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\mu}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\beta)\nu}-\frac{1}{2}g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{\alpha}(g_{0})_{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\beta}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\quad-g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{\rho}(g_{0})_{\alpha\nu}\partial_{\sigma}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\beta\mu}+g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{\rho}(g_{0})_{\sigma\alpha}\partial_{\mu}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\nu\beta}

\displaystyle\quad+g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{(\alpha}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\mu}(g_{0})_{\beta)\nu}-\frac{1}{2}g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{\alpha}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\beta}(g_{0})_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\quad-g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{\rho}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\alpha\nu}\partial_{\sigma}(g_{0})_{\beta\mu}+g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{\rho}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\sigma\alpha}\partial_{\mu}(g_{0})_{\nu\beta}

\displaystyle=-L_{0}^{\rho}g_{0}^{\mu\sigma}\left(\partial_{(\alpha}(g_{0})_{\rho\sigma}+\partial_{\rho}(g_{0})_{\sigma(\alpha}-\partial_{\sigma}(g_{0})_{(\alpha\rho}\right)\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\beta)\mu}

\displaystyle\quad+g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{(\alpha}u_{0}\left(\partial_{\rho}(g_{0})_{\beta)\sigma}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\beta)}(g_{0})_{\rho\sigma}\right)\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=-2L_{0}^{\rho}\Gamma(g_{0})^{\mu}_{(\alpha\rho}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\beta)\mu}+g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{(\alpha}u_{0}\left(\partial_{\rho}(g_{0})_{\beta)\sigma}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\beta)}(g_{0})_{\rho\sigma}\right)\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\mu\nu}.

If we now use (
4.2
) we obtain

\displaystyle B_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=-\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\left(-2L_{0}^{\rho}\Gamma(g_{0})^{\mu}_{(\alpha\rho}F^{(1)}_{\beta)\mu}+g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{(\alpha}u_{0}\left(\partial_{\rho}(g_{0})_{\beta)\sigma}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\beta)}(g_{0})_{\rho\sigma}\right)F^{(1)}_{\mu\nu}\right).

For
C_{\alpha\beta}
, we have from (
3.2
)

\displaystyle C_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{(\alpha}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\mu}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\beta)\nu}-\frac{1}{2}g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{\alpha}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\beta}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\quad-g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{\rho}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\alpha\nu}\partial_{\sigma}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\beta\mu}+g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{\rho}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\sigma\alpha}\partial_{\mu}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\nu\beta}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left|\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}\right|^{2}_{g_{0}}\partial_{\alpha}u_{0}\partial_{\beta}u_{0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\left|F^{(1)}\right|^{2}_{g_{0}}\partial_{\alpha}u_{0}\partial_{\beta}u_{0},

where we used (
2.4
) and (
4.9
). This proves (
4.18
). Similarly, we have schematically

\displaystyle P^{(1)}

\displaystyle=(g^{-1}\partial g)^{(0)}(g^{-1}\partial g)^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{g_{0}^{-1}(g^{-1})^{(1)}(\partial g_{0})^{2}}_{\vcentcolon=D}+\underbrace{g_{0}^{-1}(g^{-1})^{(1)}\partial g_{0}\partial u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}}_{\vcentcolon=E}+\underbrace{g_{0}^{-1}(g^{-1})^{(1)}(\partial u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)})^{2}}_{\vcentcolon=F}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{g_{0}^{-2}\partial g_{0}\tilde{\partial}g^{(1)}}_{\vcentcolon=G}+\underbrace{g_{0}^{-2}\partial g_{0}\partial u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(2)}}_{\vcentcolon=H}+\underbrace{g_{0}^{-2}\partial u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}\tilde{\partial}g^{(1)}}_{\vcentcolon=I}+\underbrace{g_{0}^{-2}\partial u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}\partial u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(2)}}_{\vcentcolon=J},

where we used
(\partial g)^{(1)}=\tilde{\partial}g^{(1)}+\partial u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(2)}
. For
D
,
E
,
G
and
I
, we simply look for a schematic expression using (
4.2
) and (
4.5
):

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle=\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)g_{0}^{-3}F^{(1)}(\partial g_{0})^{2},

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle=\sin\left(\frac{2u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)g_{0}^{-3}(F^{(1)})^{2}\partial g_{0}\partial u_{0},

\displaystyle G

\displaystyle=\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)g_{0}^{-2}\partial g_{0}\partial F^{(1)},

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=\sin\left(\frac{2u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)g_{0}^{-2}\partial u_{0}F^{(1)}\partial F^{(1)}.

For
F_{\alpha\beta}
, we have from (
3.2
), (
2.4
), (
4.5
) and (
4.9
)

\displaystyle F_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=g_{0}^{\mu\rho}(g^{(1)})^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{(\alpha}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\mu}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\beta)\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\left(g_{0}^{\mu\rho}(g^{(1)})^{\nu\sigma}+(g^{(1)})^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\right)\partial_{\alpha}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\beta}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\sin^{2}\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\partial_{(\alpha}u_{0}\bigg(-g_{0}^{\mu\rho}(F^{(1)})^{\nu\sigma}F^{(1)}_{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\mu}u_{0}F^{(1)}_{\beta)\nu}

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 142.26378pt+\frac{1}{4}\left(g_{0}^{\mu\rho}(F^{(1)})^{\nu\sigma}+(F^{(1)})^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\right)F^{(1)}_{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\beta)}u_{0}F^{(1)}_{\mu\nu}\right).

We now look at the terms depending on
g^{(2)}
, i.e
H
and
J
. The term
H
is actually equivalent to the term
B
above, therefore we have

\displaystyle H_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=-2L_{0}^{\rho}\Gamma(g_{0})^{\mu}_{(\alpha\rho}\partial_{\theta}g^{(2)}_{\beta)\mu}+g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{(\alpha}u_{0}\left(\partial_{\rho}(g_{0})_{\beta)\sigma}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\beta)}(g_{0})_{\rho\sigma}\right)\partial_{\theta}g^{(2)}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\bigg(-2L_{0}^{\rho}\Gamma(g_{0})^{\mu}_{(\alpha\rho}(\mathfrak{F}+F^{(2,1)})_{\beta)\mu}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{(\alpha}u_{0}\left(\partial_{\rho}(g_{0})_{\beta)\sigma}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\beta)}(g_{0})_{\rho\sigma}\right)(\mathfrak{F}+F^{(2,1)})_{\mu\nu}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad-2\sin\left(\frac{2u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\left(-2L_{0}^{\rho}\Gamma(g_{0})^{\mu}_{(\alpha\rho}F^{(2,2)}_{\beta)\mu}+g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{(\alpha}u_{0}\left(\partial_{\rho}(g_{0})_{\beta)\sigma}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\beta)}(g_{0})_{\rho\sigma}\right)F^{(2,2)}_{\mu\nu}\right),

where we used (
4.3
). Finally for
J
, we have from (
3.2
), (
2.4
) and (
4.9
)

\displaystyle J_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{(\alpha}u_{0}\left(\partial_{\mu}u_{0}\left(\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\theta}g^{(2)}_{\beta)\nu}+\partial_{\theta}g^{(2)}_{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\beta)\nu}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\beta)}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(1)}_{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\theta}g^{(2)}_{\mu\nu}\right)

\displaystyle=-\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{(\alpha}u_{0}\left(\partial_{\mu}u_{0}\left(F^{(1)}_{\rho\sigma}\mathfrak{F}_{\beta)\nu}+\mathfrak{F}_{\rho\sigma}F^{(1)}_{\beta)\nu}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\beta)}u_{0}F^{(1)}_{\rho\sigma}\mathfrak{F}_{\mu\nu}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{(\alpha}u_{0}\left(\partial_{\mu}u_{0}\left(F^{(1)}_{\rho\sigma}F^{(2,1)}_{\beta)\nu}+F^{(2,1)}_{\rho\sigma}F^{(1)}_{\beta)\nu}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\beta)}u_{0}F^{(1)}_{\rho\sigma}F^{(2,1)}_{\mu\nu}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+2\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)g_{0}^{\mu\rho}g_{0}^{\nu\sigma}\partial_{(\alpha}u_{0}\left(\partial_{\mu}u_{0}\left(F^{(1)}_{\rho\sigma}F^{(2,2)}_{\beta)\nu}+F^{(2,2)}_{\rho\sigma}F^{(1)}_{\beta)\nu}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\beta)}u_{0}F^{(1)}_{\rho\sigma}F^{(2,2)}_{\mu\nu}\right),

where we also used (
4.2
) and (
4.3
). This concludes the proof of (
4.19
).

A.3
Proof of Proposition
4.3

The gauge term
H^{\rho}=g^{\mu\nu}\Gamma^{\rho}_{\mu\nu}
is of the form
g^{-2}\partial g
. In order to expand
H^{\rho}
, we will first expand
\partial g
and then
g^{-2}
. More precisely, we have

\displaystyle H^{\rho}

\displaystyle=g^{\rho\sigma}g^{\mu\nu}\left(\partial_{\mu}g_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\sigma}g_{\mu\nu}\right).

For convenience, let us define

\displaystyle H^{\rho}\left[T\right]

\displaystyle=g^{\rho\sigma}g^{\mu\nu}\left(\partial_{\mu}T_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\sigma}T_{\mu\nu}\right),

for
T
a symmetric 2-tensor. With this notation, we have

\displaystyle H^{\rho}

\displaystyle=H^{\rho}[g_{0}]+\lambda H^{\rho}\left[g^{(1)}\right]+\lambda^{2}H^{\rho}\left[g^{(2)}\right]+\lambda^{2}H^{\rho}\left[\mathfrak{h}\right]+\lambda^{3}H^{\rho}\left[g^{(3)}\right].

We expand each term in this expression. For
g_{0}
, we use (
2.7
) and obtain

\displaystyle H^{\rho}[g_{0}]

\displaystyle=g^{\rho\sigma}g_{0}^{\mu\nu}\left(\partial_{\mu}(g_{0})_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\sigma}(g_{0})_{\mu\nu}\right)+\lambda g^{\rho\sigma}(g^{\mu\nu})^{(\geq 1)}\left(\partial_{\mu}(g_{0})_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\sigma}(g_{0})_{\mu\nu}\right)

\displaystyle=\lambda g^{\rho\sigma}(g^{\mu\nu})^{(\geq 1)}\left(\partial_{\mu}(g_{0})_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\sigma}(g_{0})_{\mu\nu}\right).

We now use further the expansion of the inverse (especially (
4.6
)) and obtain

\displaystyle H^{\rho}[g_{0}]

\displaystyle=\lambda\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)g_{0}^{-3}F^{(1)}\partial g_{0}-\lambda^{2}g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\nu}\left(\partial_{\mu}(g_{0})_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\sigma}(g_{0})_{\mu\nu}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda^{2}g_{0}^{-3}\left((1+g_{0}^{-1})(F^{(1)})^{2}+F^{(2,i)}+\mathfrak{F}\right)\partial g_{0}

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda^{3}\left(g^{-1}(g^{-1})^{(\geq 3)}\partial g_{0}+(g^{-1})^{(\geq 2)}(g^{-1})^{(1)}\partial g_{0}+(g^{-1})^{(\geq 1)}(g^{\mu\nu})^{(2)}\partial g_{0}\right).

If
\mathrm{T}
is an oscillating function and
S
a symmetric 2-tensor then

\displaystyle H^{\rho}\left[\mathrm{T}\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)S\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)g^{\rho\sigma}g^{\mu\nu}\left(\partial_{\mu}u_{0}S_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\sigma}u_{0}S_{\mu\nu}\right)+\mathrm{T}\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)H^{\rho}\left[S\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)g^{\rho\sigma}\mathrm{Pol}_{\sigma}(S)+\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)g^{\rho\sigma}(g^{\mu\nu})^{(\geq 1)}\left(\partial_{\mu}u_{0}S_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\sigma}u_{0}S_{\mu\nu}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathrm{T}\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)H^{\rho}\left[S\right].

Using also
\mathrm{Pol}\left(F^{(1)}\right)=0
and
F^{(1)}_{L_{0}\alpha}=0
, this gives

\displaystyle\lambda H^{\rho}\left[g^{(1)}\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{\lambda}{4}\sin\left(\frac{2u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\sigma}u_{0}\left|F^{(1)}\right|^{2}_{g_{0}}+\lambda^{2}\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\nu}\left(\partial_{\mu}u_{0}F^{(1)}_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\sigma}u_{0}F^{(1)}_{\mu\nu}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)g^{-2}\partial F^{(1)}

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda^{2}\left(g_{0}^{-3}\left((F^{(1)})^{2}+F^{(2,i)}+\mathfrak{F}\right)\partial u_{0}F^{(1)}+(g^{-1})^{(\geq 1)}(g^{-1})^{(1)}\partial u_{0}F^{(1)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda^{3}\left(g^{-1}(g^{-1})^{(\geq 3)}\partial u_{0}F^{(1)}+(g^{-1})^{(\geq 1)}(g^{-1})^{(2)}\partial u_{0}F^{(1)}\right),

and

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}H^{\rho}\left[g^{(2)}\right]

\displaystyle=\lambda\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\left(g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\mathrm{Pol}_{\sigma}\left(\mathfrak{F}\right)+g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\mathrm{Pol}_{\sigma}\left(F^{(2,1)}\right)\right)-\lambda\sin\left(\frac{2u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\mathrm{Pol}_{\sigma}\left(F^{(2,2)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda^{2}\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)H^{\rho}\left[\mathfrak{F}\right]+\lambda^{2}\left(g^{-2}\partial F^{(2,i)}+(g^{-1})^{(\geq 1)}g^{-1}\partial u_{0}(\mathfrak{F}+F^{(2,i)})\right),

and

\displaystyle\lambda^{3}H^{\rho}\left[g^{(3)}\right]

\displaystyle=\lambda^{2}g^{\rho\sigma}\mathrm{Pol}_{\sigma}\left(\partial_{\theta}g^{(3)}\right)+\lambda^{3}H^{\rho}\left[\tilde{\partial}g^{(3)}\right]+\lambda^{3}g^{-1}(g^{-1})^{(\geq 1)}\partial u_{0}\partial_{\theta}g^{(3)}.

Collecting terms for each power of
\lambda
in the previous expressions, we obtain the various expressions of Proposition
4.3
, using in particular the fact that

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{\rho}

\displaystyle=H^{\rho}\left[\mathfrak{h}\right]+\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)H^{\rho}\left[\mathfrak{F}\right]+\lambda H^{\rho}\left[\tilde{\partial}g^{(3)}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\nu}\left(\partial_{\mu}(g_{0})_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\sigma}(g_{0})_{\mu\nu}-\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\left(\partial_{\mu}u_{0}F^{(1)}_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\sigma}u_{0}F^{(1)}_{\mu\nu}\right)\right).

A.4
Proof of Proposition
4.4

From (
3.1
) we obtain

\displaystyle 2R^{(0)}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=-W^{(0)}_{\alpha\beta}+P^{(0)}_{\alpha\beta}+(g_{0})_{\rho(\alpha}\partial_{\beta)}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}(H^{(1)})^{\rho},

where we used (
4.24
) to compute
\left(H^{\rho}\partial_{\rho}g_{\alpha\beta}+g_{\rho(\alpha}\partial_{\beta)}H^{\rho}\right)^{(0)}
. We now use (
4.11
), (
4.18
) and (
4.25
) to obtain

\displaystyle 2R^{(0)}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=-\tilde{\Box}_{g_{0}}(g_{0})_{\alpha\beta}+\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\left(-2L_{0}F^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}+(\tilde{\Box}_{g_{0}}u_{0})F^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+P_{\alpha\beta}(g_{0})(\partial g_{0},\partial g_{0})-\frac{1}{4}\left|F^{(1)}\right|^{2}_{g_{0}}\partial_{\alpha}u_{0}\partial_{\beta}u_{0}

\displaystyle\quad-\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\left(-2L_{0}^{\rho}\Gamma(g_{0})^{\nu}_{(\alpha\rho}F^{(1)}_{\beta)\nu}+\partial_{(\alpha}u_{0}\hat{P}^{(0,1)}_{\beta)}\right)+\frac{1}{4}\cos\left(\frac{2u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\left|F^{(1)}\right|^{2}_{g_{0}}\partial_{\alpha}u_{0}\partial_{\beta}u_{0}

\displaystyle\quad-\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\partial_{(\alpha}u_{0}\left(\mathrm{Pol}_{\beta)}\left(F^{(2,1)}\right)+\mathrm{Pol}_{\beta)}\left(\mathfrak{F}\right)+(g_{0})_{\rho\beta)}(\tilde{H}^{(1,1)})^{\rho}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+2\cos\left(\frac{2u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\partial_{(\alpha}u_{0}\left(-2\mathrm{Pol}_{\beta)}\left(F^{(2,2)}\right)-\frac{1}{4}\partial_{\beta)}u_{0}\left|F^{(1)}\right|^{2}_{g_{0}}\right).

Regrouping terms with respect to their oscillation behaviour concludes the proof of (
4.33
). Similarly, from (
3.1
) we obtain

\displaystyle 2R^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=-W^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}+P^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}+\left(H^{\rho}\partial_{\rho}g_{\alpha\beta}+g_{\rho(\alpha}\partial_{\beta)}H^{\rho}\right)^{(1)}.

Using (
4.24
) we compute the last term:

\displaystyle\left(H^{\rho}\partial_{\rho}g_{\alpha\beta}+g_{\rho(\alpha}\partial_{\beta)}H^{\rho}\right)^{(1)}

\displaystyle=(H^{(1)})^{\rho}\left(\partial_{\rho}(g_{0})_{\alpha\beta}-\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\partial_{\rho}u_{0}F^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+(g_{0})_{\rho(\alpha}\left(\tilde{\partial}_{\beta)}(H^{(1)})^{\rho}+\partial_{\beta)}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}\left((H^{(2)})^{\rho}+(\Upsilon^{\rho})^{(0)}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)F^{(1)}_{\rho(\alpha}\partial_{\beta)}u_{0}\partial_{\theta}(H^{(1)})^{\rho}.

We now use (
4.12
), (
4.19
), (
4.25
) and (
4.26
) to obtain

\displaystyle 2R^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=-\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\left(-2L_{0}F^{(2,1)}_{\alpha\beta}+(\tilde{\Box}_{g_{0}}u_{0})F^{(2,1)}_{\alpha\beta}+\tilde{W}^{(1,1)}_{\alpha\beta}-2L_{0}\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha\beta}+(\tilde{\Box}_{g_{0}}u_{0})\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha\beta}+\mathfrak{h}_{L_{0}L_{0}}F^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+2\sin\left(\frac{2u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\left(-2L_{0}F^{(2,2)}_{\alpha\beta}+(\tilde{\Box}_{g_{0}}u_{0})F^{(2,2)}_{\alpha\beta}+\tilde{W}^{(1,2)}_{\alpha\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\sin\left(\frac{2u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\mathfrak{F}_{L_{0}L_{0}}F^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}-\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\cos\left(\frac{2u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)F^{(2,2)}_{L_{0}L_{0}}F^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\quad+\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\left(-2L_{0}^{\rho}\Gamma(g_{0})^{\nu}_{(\alpha\rho}\left(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu\beta)}+F^{(2,1)}_{\nu\beta)}\right)+P^{(1,1)}_{\alpha\beta}+\partial_{(\alpha}u_{0}\hat{P}^{(1,1)}_{\beta)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sin\left(\frac{2u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\left(4L_{0}^{\rho}\Gamma(g_{0})^{\nu}_{(\alpha\rho}F^{(2,2)}_{\nu\beta)}+P^{(1,2)}_{\alpha\beta}+\partial_{(\alpha}u_{0}\hat{P}^{(1,2)}_{\beta)}\right)+\cos\left(\frac{3u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\partial_{(\alpha}u_{0}\hat{P}_{\beta)}^{(1,3)}

\displaystyle\quad+\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\left(g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\mathrm{Pol}_{\sigma}\left(F^{(2,1)}\right)+g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\mathrm{Pol}_{\sigma}\left(\mathfrak{F}\right)+(\tilde{H}^{(1,1)})^{\rho}\right)\left(\partial_{\rho}(g_{0})_{\alpha\beta}-\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\partial_{\rho}u_{0}F^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sin\left(\frac{2u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\left(-2g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\mathrm{Pol}_{\sigma}\left(F^{(2,2)}\right)-\frac{1}{4}g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\sigma}u_{0}\left|F^{(1)}\right|^{2}_{g_{0}}\right)\left(\partial_{\rho}(g_{0})_{\alpha\beta}-\sin\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\partial_{\rho}u_{0}F^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)(g_{0})_{\rho(\alpha}\partial_{\beta)}\left(g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\mathrm{Pol}_{\sigma}\left(F^{(2,1)}\right)+g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\mathrm{Pol}_{\sigma}\left(\mathfrak{F}\right)+(\tilde{H}^{(1,1)})^{\rho}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sin\left(\frac{2u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)(g_{0})_{\rho(\alpha}\partial_{\beta)}\left(-2g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\mathrm{Pol}_{\sigma}\left(F^{(2,2)}\right)-\frac{1}{4}g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\sigma}u_{0}\left|F^{(1)}\right|^{2}_{g_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\partial_{(\alpha}u_{0}\left(\mathrm{Pol}_{\beta)}\left(\partial_{\theta}^{2}g^{(3)}\right)+(g_{0})_{\rho\beta)}\partial_{\theta}(\tilde{H}^{(2)})^{\rho}+(g_{0})_{\rho\beta)}\partial_{\theta}(\Upsilon^{\rho})^{(0)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\sin\left(\frac{2u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)F^{(1)}_{\rho(\alpha}\partial_{\beta)}u_{0}\left(g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\mathrm{Pol}_{\sigma}\left(F^{(2,1)}\right)+g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\mathrm{Pol}_{\sigma}\left(\mathfrak{F}\right)+(\tilde{H}^{(1,1)})^{\rho}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+2\cos\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)\cos\left(\frac{2u_{0}}{\lambda}\right)F^{(1)}_{\rho(\alpha}\partial_{\beta)}u_{0}\left(-2g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\mathrm{Pol}_{\sigma}\left(F^{(2,2)}\right)-\frac{1}{4}g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\sigma}u_{0}\left|F^{(1)}\right|^{2}_{g_{0}}\right).

We regroup terms with respect to their oscillation behaviour and use the following fact to conclude the proof of (
4.34
):

\displaystyle g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\Omega_{\sigma}\partial_{\rho}(g_{0})_{\alpha\beta}+(g_{0})_{\rho(\alpha}\partial_{\beta)}\left(g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\Omega_{\sigma}\right)=\mathbf{D}_{(\alpha}\Omega_{\beta)},

(A.5)

where
\Omega
is any 1-tensor. We conclude the proof by proving (
4.35
). From (
3.1
) we obtain

\displaystyle 2R^{(\geq 2)}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=-W^{(\geq 2)}_{\alpha\beta}+P^{(\geq 2)}_{\alpha\beta}+\left(H^{\rho}\partial_{\rho}g_{\alpha\beta}+g_{\rho(\alpha}\partial_{\beta)}H^{\rho}\right)^{(\geq 2)},

and we simply use (
4.13
) to conclude.

Appendix B
The commutator
[\Box_{g_{0}},L_{0}]
and proof of Lemma
3.1

This appendix is devoted to the proof of Lemma
3.1
, which estimates the commutator
[\Box_{g_{0}},L_{0}]
, where
L_{0}
is defined by (
2.6
) and
g_{0}
is the background metric. A general computation using the expression of the wave operator in coordinates shows that if
X
is any vector field and
f
a scalar function we have

\displaystyle[\Box_{g_{0}},X]f

\displaystyle=2g_{0}^{\alpha\beta}g_{0}^{\mu\nu}(\mathbf{D}_{\alpha}X)_{\mu}\partial_{\beta}\partial_{\nu}f+\partial_{\alpha}f\left(\Box_{g_{0}}X^{\alpha}+X\left(g_{0}^{\rho\sigma}\Gamma(g_{0})^{\alpha}_{\rho\sigma}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=2g_{0}^{\alpha\beta}g_{0}^{\mu\nu}(\mathbf{D}_{\alpha}X)_{\mu}\partial_{\beta}\partial_{\nu}f+\partial_{\alpha}f\Box_{g_{0}}X^{\alpha},

(B.1)

where we also used the wave coordinate condition for the background (
2.7
). In the context of Lemma
3.1
, we consider
X=L_{0}
and
f
compactly supported and we are only interested in the principal terms in
[\Box_{g_{0}},L_{0}]f
. Indeed the non-principal part simply satisfies

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{\alpha}f\Box_{g_{0}}L_{0}^{\alpha}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim|\partial f|.

(B.2)

with a constant only depending on the background spacetime. It remains to estimate the second derivatives of
f
in (
B.1
), i.e

\displaystyle g_{0}^{\alpha\beta}g_{0}^{\mu\nu}(\mathbf{D}_{\alpha}L_{0})_{\mu}\partial_{\beta}\partial_{\nu}f,

by using the expression of the background metric in the background null frame defined in Section
2.2
:

\displaystyle g_{0}^{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}L_{0}^{(\alpha}\underline{L}_{0}^{\beta)}+\delta^{AB}e_{A}^{\alpha}e_{B}^{\beta}.

(B.3)

Using (
B.3
) twice and the fact that
L_{0}
is geodesic, that is
\mathbf{D}_{L_{0}}L_{0}=0
, we obtain

\displaystyle g_{0}^{\alpha\beta}g_{0}^{\mu\nu}(\mathbf{D}_{\alpha}L_{0})_{\mu}\partial_{\beta}\partial_{\nu}f

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}g_{0}^{\mu\nu}(\mathbf{D}_{L_{0}}L_{0})_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\underline{L}_{0}f-\frac{1}{2}g_{0}^{\mu\nu}(\mathbf{D}_{\underline{L}_{0}}L_{0})_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}L_{0}f

\displaystyle\quad+\delta^{AB}g_{0}^{\mu\nu}(\mathbf{D}_{e_{A}}L_{0})_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}e_{B}f

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}g_{0}^{\mu\nu}(\mathbf{D}_{\underline{L}_{0}}L_{0})_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}L_{0}f-\frac{1}{2}\delta^{AB}L_{0}^{\mu}(\mathbf{D}_{e_{A}}L_{0})_{\mu}\underline{L}_{0}e_{B}f

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\delta^{AB}\underline{L}_{0}^{\mu}(\mathbf{D}_{e_{A}}L_{0})_{\mu}L_{0}e_{B}f+\delta^{AB}\delta^{CD}e_{C}^{\mu}(\mathbf{D}_{e_{A}}L_{0})_{\mu}e_{D}e_{B}f

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}g_{0}^{\mu\nu}(\mathbf{D}_{\underline{L}_{0}}L_{0})_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}L_{0}f-\frac{1}{2}\delta^{AB}\underline{L}_{0}^{\mu}(\mathbf{D}_{e_{A}}L_{0})_{\mu}L_{0}e_{B}f

\displaystyle\quad+\delta^{AB}\delta^{CD}e_{C}^{\mu}(\mathbf{D}_{e_{A}}L_{0})_{\mu}e_{D}e_{B}f,

where we also used
L_{0}^{\mu}(\mathbf{D}_{e_{A}}L_{0})_{\mu}=0
, which follows from the fact
g_{0}(L_{0},L_{0})=0
is constant. Using now the fact that
\left|L_{0}e_{B}f\right|\lesssim|\partial L_{0}f|+|\partial f|
and recalling (
B.1
) and (
B.2
) we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|[\Box_{g_{0}},L_{0}]f\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|e_{A}e_{B}f\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\partial L_{0}f\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\partial f\right\|_{L^{2}},

(B.4)

where we also used the compact support of
f
to switch from pointwise estimates to
L^{2}
norms.
Therefore, in order to prove Lemma
3.1
it remains to estimate
\left\|e_{A}e_{B}f\right\|_{L^{2}}
. This follows from elliptic estimates on
P_{t,u}
. If
K
denotes the Gauss curvature of
P_{t,u}
and if
\mathrm{d}\mu_{t,u}
denotes the volume form on
P_{t,u}
induced by
\mathring{g}_{0}
, then the scalar Bochner identity (Proposition 3.5 in
[
Sze18
]
) reads

\displaystyle\int_{P_{t,u}}\left|\mathrm{Hess}f\right|^{2}_{\mathring{g}_{0}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{t,u}

\displaystyle=\int_{P_{t,u}}(\mbox{$\Delta\mkern-13.0mu/$\,}f)^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{t,u}-\int_{P_{t,u}}K|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\,}f|^{2}_{\mathring{g}_{0}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{t,u}.

(B.5)

where

\displaystyle\mbox{$\Delta\mkern-13.0mu/$\,}f

\displaystyle=\delta^{AB}e_{A}e_{B}f+\delta^{AB}\delta^{CD}g(\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\,}_{e_{A}}e_{C},e_{B})e_{D}f,

\displaystyle\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\,}f

\displaystyle=\delta^{AB}(e_{A}f)e_{B}.

Thanks to the regularity assumptions stated in Section
2.2
and the fact that the 2-surfaces
P_{t,u}
foliates
\Sigma_{t}
, we can integrate (
B.5
) in the
N_{0}
direction and obtain

\displaystyle\left\|e_{A}e_{B}f\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\left\|\mbox{$\Delta\mkern-13.0mu/$\,}f\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\partial f\right\|_{L^{2}},

where we recall that
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{L^{2}}
denotes
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{t})}
and where the implicit constant depends only on background quantities. Together with (
B.4
) this gives

\displaystyle\left\|[\Box_{g_{0}},L_{0}]f\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\mbox{$\Delta\mkern-13.0mu/$\,}f\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\partial L_{0}f\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\partial f\right\|_{L^{2}}.

(B.6)

We now use Lemma 2.5 of
[
Sze12
]
which gives the expression of the wave operator in the null frame and implies

\displaystyle\left\|\mbox{$\Delta\mkern-13.0mu/$\,}f\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\left\|\Box_{g_{0}}f\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\partial L_{0}f\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\partial f\right\|_{L^{2}}.

Together with (
B.6
) this concludes the proof of the first part of Lemma
3.1
.

The second part is proved in a similar way. For
r\geq 1
, we apply (
3.32
) to
\nabla^{r}f
and obtain

\displaystyle\left\|[L_{0},\Box_{g_{0}}]\nabla^{r}f\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\partial L_{0}\nabla^{r}f\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\Box_{g_{0}}\nabla^{r}f\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\partial f\right\|_{H^{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla^{r}\partial L_{0}f\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla^{r}\Box_{g_{0}}f\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\partial f\right\|_{H^{r}}+\left\|[\partial L_{0},\nabla^{r}]f\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|[\Box_{g_{0}},\nabla^{r}]f\right\|_{L^{2}}.

Moreover we have

\displaystyle\left\|[\partial L_{0},\nabla^{r}]f\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|[\Box_{g_{0}},\nabla^{r}]f\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\left\|\partial^{2}f\right\|_{H^{r-1}},

where we recall that
r\geq 1
. It remains to notice that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{r}[L_{0},\Box_{g_{0}}]f\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\left\|[L_{0},\Box_{g_{0}}]\nabla^{r}f\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\partial f\right\|_{H^{r}}+\left\|\partial^{2}f\right\|_{H^{r-1}}.

This concludes the proof of Lemma
3.1
.

Appendix C
The spectral projections

In this section, we prove Lemma
3.2
. The proof is based on the dyadic decomposition at the heart of Littlewood-Paley theory, which we present shortly.

C.1
Littlewood-Paley theory

This presentation of the Littlewood-Paley theory is based on
[
BCD11
]
. We start by considering two smooth radial functions
\chi
and
\varphi
from
\mathbb{R}^{3}
to the interval
[0,1]
supported in
\left\{|\xi|\leq\frac{4}{3}\right\}
and
\left\{\frac{3}{4}\leq|\xi|\leq\frac{8}{3}\right\}
respectively and such that

\displaystyle\chi+\sum_{j\geq 0}\varphi\left(2^{-j}\cdot\right)=1,

(C.1)

\displaystyle|j-i|\geq 2\Longrightarrow\mathrm{supp}\left(\varphi\left(2^{-j}\cdot\right)\right)\cap\mathrm{supp}\left(\varphi\left(2^{-i}\cdot\right)\right)=\emptyset,

\displaystyle j\geq 1\Longrightarrow\mathrm{supp}\left(\chi\right)\cap\mathrm{supp}\left(\varphi\left(2^{-j}\cdot\right)\right)=\emptyset,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\leq\chi^{2}+\sum_{j\geq 0}\varphi^{2}\left(2^{-j}\cdot\right)\leq 1.

(C.2)

The existence of
\chi
and
\varphi
is the content of Proposition 2.10 in
[
BCD11
]
. They define a dyadic partition of unity, with which we can define the dyadic blocks
\Delta_{j}
. If
u
is a tempered distribution on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
we set
\Delta_{i}u=0
if
i\leq-2
and

\displaystyle\Delta_{-1}u

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\chi\mathcal{F}(u)\right),

\displaystyle\Delta_{j}u

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\varphi\left(2^{-j}\cdot\right)\mathcal{F}(u)\right),

for
j\geq 0
and where
\mathcal{F}
denotes the Fourier transform on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. We also define

\displaystyle S_{j}u=\sum_{j^{\prime}\leq j-1}\Delta_{j^{\prime}}u,

for
j\geq 0
. For
u
a tempered distribution, we use the notation
\mathrm{spect}\left(u\right)=\mathrm{supp}\left(\mathcal{F}(u)\right)
. Therefore the support properties of
\chi
and
\varphi
imply

\displaystyle\mathrm{spect}\left(\Delta_{-1}u\right)

\displaystyle\subset\left\{|\xi|\leq\frac{4}{3}\right\},

(C.3)

\displaystyle\mathrm{spect}\left(\Delta_{j}u\right)

\displaystyle\subset 2^{j}\left\{\frac{3}{4}\leq|\xi|\leq\frac{8}{3}\right\},

(C.4)

for
j\geq 0
. The following proposition contains all the basic estimates we need on the dyadic blocks.

Proposition C.1
.

Let
u
be a tempered distribution.

(i)

The so-called Bernstein estimates hold

\displaystyle\left\|X_{j}\nabla^{\alpha}u\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\alpha j}\left\|X_{j}u\right\|_{L^{p}},

(C.5)

for
X_{j}\in\{S_{j},\Delta_{j}\}
,
\alpha=0,1
and for all
1\leq p\leq+\infty
.

(ii)

Moreover, the following estimates hold

\displaystyle\left\|X_{j}u\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{\frac{3}{2}j}\left\|X_{j}u\right\|_{L^{2}},

(C.6)

for
X_{j}\in\{S_{j},\Delta_{j}\}
.

Proof.

For
j\geq-1
, we have
\mathrm{spect}\left(X_{j}\nabla u\right)\subset 2^{j}B
for
B
a ball centered at 0 in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Let
\phi
be a compactly supported function on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
such that
\phi\restriction{B}=1
. This implies

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left(X_{j}u\right)=\phi\left(2^{-j}\cdot\right)\mathcal{F}\left(X_{j}u\right).

Therefore, for
\alpha=0,1
we have

\displaystyle X_{j}\nabla^{\alpha}u=2^{(3+\alpha)j}\left(\nabla^{\alpha}\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\phi)\right)\left(2^{j}\cdot\right)*X_{j}u,

(C.7)

where the symbol
*
denotes the convolution between two functions. The Young inequality implies

\displaystyle\left\|X_{j}\nabla^{\alpha}u\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{(3+\alpha)j}\left\|\left(\nabla^{\alpha}\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\phi)\right)\left(2^{j}\cdot\right)\right\|_{L^{1}}\left\|X_{j}u\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\alpha j}\left\|\nabla^{\alpha}\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\phi)\right\|_{L^{1}}\left\|X_{j}u\right\|_{L^{p}},

where we change variable in the last step. This proves (
C.5
). Now, if
\alpha=0
in (
C.7
), another case of Young inequality implies

\displaystyle\left\|X_{j}u\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{3j}\left\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\phi)\left(2^{j}\cdot\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|X_{j}u\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\frac{3}{2}j}\left\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\phi)\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|X_{j}u\right\|_{L^{2}},

which concludes the proof of the proposition.
∎

The property (
C.1
) allows us to decompose each function
u
into an infinite sum of smooth and localized in Fourier space functions:

\displaystyle u=\sum_{j\geq-1}\Delta_{j}u.

(C.8)

The series (
C.8
) is
a priori
purely formal, and though each dyadic blocks
\Delta_{j}u
is smooth the function
u
might have to be defined merely as a distribution. However, one can link the regularity of
u
and the summability properties of the series. The next proposition gives a characterization of the Sobolev spaces in terms of the convergence of the series (
C.8
).

Proposition C.2
.

If
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
u
is a tempered distribution, then

\displaystyle u\in H^{s}\iff\left(2^{js}\left\|\Delta_{j}u\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in\ell^{2}(\mathbb{N}).

Moreover, there exists
C>0
such that for all tempered distribution
u
we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{C}\sum_{j\geq-1}2^{2js}\left\|\Delta_{j}u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\left\|u\right\|^{2}_{H^{s}}\leq C\sum_{j\geq-1}2^{2js}\left\|\Delta_{j}u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

(C.9)

Proof.

By definition of the space
H^{s}
, we have
\left\|u\right\|_{H^{s}}=\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{s}\mathcal{F}(u)\right\|_{L^{2}}
. Using the first inequality in (
C.2
) we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|u\right\|^{2}_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\langle\xi\rangle^{2s}\chi^{2}(\xi)|\mathcal{F}(u)(\xi)|^{2}\mathrm{d}\xi+\sum_{j\geq 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\langle\xi\rangle^{2s}\varphi^{2}\left(2^{-j}\xi\right)|\mathcal{F}(u)(\xi)|^{2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-2s}\left\|\Delta_{-1}u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sum_{j\geq 0}2^{2js}\left\|\Delta_{j}u\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

where we used the support properties of
\chi
and
\varphi
. This proves half of (
C.9
), the other half is proved similarly using the other inequality in (
C.2
).
∎

The decomposition (
C.8
) is also very useful to study products of functions. Indeed, formally we have

\displaystyle uv=\sum_{j,j^{\prime}\geq-1}\Delta_{j}u\Delta_{j^{\prime}}v

for
u
and
v
two tempered distributions. As it is well known the product
uv
could not even be a tempered distribution. In order to understand the product’s properties, Bony introduced in
[
Bon81
]
what is now called the Bony decomposition:

Definition C.1
.

For
u
and
v
two tempered distributions, we define the paraproduct
T_{u}v
and the remainder
R(u,v)
by

\displaystyle T_{u}v

\displaystyle=\sum_{j}S_{j-1}u\Delta_{j}v,

\displaystyle R(u,v)

\displaystyle=\sum_{|j-k|\leq 1}\Delta_{j}u\Delta_{k}v.

The Bony decomposition of
uv
is then

\displaystyle uv=T_{u}v+T_{v}u+R(u,v).

In the sequel, we don’t directly use Bony’s decomposition but rather the spectral property of the general terms in the series defining
T_{u}v
and
R(u,v)
, namely if
i\geq-1
and
|j-k|\leq 1
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{spect}\left(S_{i-1}u\Delta_{i}v\right)

\displaystyle\subset 2^{i}\mathcal{C}^{\prime},

(C.10)

\displaystyle\mathrm{spect}\left(\Delta_{j}u\Delta_{k}v\right)

\displaystyle\subset 2^{j}B^{\prime},

(C.11)

with
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}=\left\{\frac{1}{12}\leq|\xi|\leq 3\right\}
and
B^{\prime}=\left\{0\leq|\xi|\leq\frac{32}{3}\right\}
.

C.2
Properties of
\Pi_{\leq}
and
\Pi_{\geq}

Recall Definition
5.1
, where we define the operators
\Pi_{\leq}
and
\Pi_{\geq}
. Their behaviour with respect to derivatives is similar to the one of dyadic blocks (see Proposition
C.1
) thanks to the support property of their symbols.

Lemma C.1
.

Let
f
be a scalar function. We have

\displaystyle\lambda\left\|\nabla\Pi_{\leq}(f)\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}},

(C.12)

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi_{\geq}(f)\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda\left\|\nabla f\right\|_{L^{2}}.

(C.13)

Proof.

To prove (
C.12
), we use Parseval’s identity twice and the support property of
\chi_{\lambda}
:

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\Pi_{\leq}(f)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\int_{|\xi|\leq\frac{2}{\lambda}}|\xi|^{2}\left|\mathcal{F}(f)(\xi)\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\left|\mathcal{F}(f)(\xi)\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}\xi=\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}}.

The proof of (
C.12
) is similar:

\displaystyle\left\|\Pi_{\geq}(f)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\lambda|\xi|\geq 1}\left|\mathcal{F}(f)(\xi)\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\lambda^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}|\xi|^{2}\left|\mathcal{F}(f)(\xi)\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}\xi=\lambda^{2}\left\|\nabla f\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

∎

The following lemma is a special case of Lemma 2.97 in
[
BCD11
]
. For the sake of completeness, we redo the proof.

Lemma C.2
.

Let
u
and
v
be two tempered distributions. We have

\displaystyle\left\|\left[u,\Pi_{\leq}\right]v\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lambda\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|v\right\|_{L^{2}}.

(C.14)

Proof.

Using the usual notation of pseudo-differential calculus we have
\Pi_{\leq}=\chi_{1}\left(\lambda\nabla\right)
. For
f
a function we have
\chi_{1}\left(\lambda\nabla\right)f=\lambda^{-3}\left(\mathcal{F}^{-1}\chi_{1}\right)\left(\lambda^{-1}\cdot\right)*f
. This gives

\displaystyle\left(\left[u,\chi_{1}\left(\lambda\nabla\right)\right]v\right)(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mathcal{F}^{-1}\chi_{1}\right)\left(\lambda^{-1}(x-y)\right)(u(y)-u(x))v(y)\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\lambda^{3}}\int_{[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\mathcal{F}^{-1}\chi_{1}\right)\left(\lambda^{-1}z\right)\langle\nabla u(x-z\tau),z\rangle v(x-z)\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}\tau,

where we used the Taylor expansion for formula for
u
. Defining
\Psi(z)=z\left(\mathcal{F}^{-1}\chi_{1}\right)(z)
we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\left(\left[u,\chi_{1}\left(\lambda\nabla\right)\right]v\right)(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{\infty}}}{\lambda^{2}}\int_{[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left|\Psi\left(\lambda^{-1}z\right)\right||v(x-z)|\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}\tau.

We take the
L^{2}
norm of this inequality to obtain after a last change of variable in
\Psi
:

\displaystyle\left\|\left[u,\chi_{1}\left(\lambda\nabla\right)\right]v\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq\lambda\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|v\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\Psi\right\|_{L^{1}}.

This concludes the proof, noting that
\left\|\Psi\right\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\left\|\chi_{1}\right\|_{C^{k}}
for
k
large enough.
∎

C.3
Proof of Lemma
3.2

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
the unique integer such that
\frac{1}{\lambda}\leq 2^{N}<\frac{2}{\lambda}
. In this proof, we will mainly use the fact that
\chi_{\lambda}(\xi)=1
if
|\xi|\leq 2^{N-1}
and
\chi_{\lambda}(\xi)=0
if
|\xi|\geq 2^{N+1}
(recall that
\chi_{\lambda}
is defined in Definition
5.1
). To benefit from this, we use the dyadic decompositions of
u
and
v
and regroup terms following the paraproduct decomposition (see Definition
C.1
) to obtain:

\displaystyle\left[u,\Pi_{\leq}\right]\nabla v

\displaystyle=\sum_{j}\left[S_{j-1}u,\Pi_{\leq}\right]\Delta_{j}\nabla v+\sum_{j}\left[\Delta_{j}u,\Pi_{\leq}\right]S_{j-1}\nabla v+\sum_{|i-j|\leq 1}\left[\Delta_{i}u,\Pi_{\leq}\right]\Delta_{j}\nabla v

\displaystyle=\vcentcolon A_{1}+A_{2}+A_{3}.

We start with
A_{1}
. We have

\displaystyle\left[S_{j-1}u,\Pi_{\leq}\right]\Delta_{j}\nabla v

\displaystyle=S_{j-1}u\Pi_{\leq}\left(\Delta_{j}\nabla v\right)-\Pi_{\leq}\left(S_{j-1}u\Delta_{j}\nabla v\right).

(C.15)

If
j
is such that
2^{j}\times\frac{1}{12}\geq 2^{N+1}
then (
C.4
) implies
\Pi_{\leq}\Delta_{j}=0
and (
C.10
) implies that
\Pi_{\leq}\left(S_{j-1}u\Delta_{j}\nabla v\right)=0
. Therefore in this case the two terms in (
C.15
) vanish. If
3\times 2^{j}\leq 2^{N-1}
then
\Pi_{\leq}\Delta_{j}=\Delta_{j}
and
\Pi_{\leq}\left(S_{j-1}u\Delta_{j}\nabla v\right)=S_{j-1}u\Delta_{j}\nabla v
, so the two terms in (
C.15
) are equal. Therefore there exists
C>0
independent from
\lambda
such that

\displaystyle\left\|A_{1}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\frac{1}{C\lambda}\leq 2^{j}\leq\frac{C}{\lambda}}\left\|\left[S_{j-1}u,\Pi_{\leq}\right]\Delta_{j}\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}.

We now use (
C.14
) and (
C.5
) to obtain

\displaystyle\left\|A_{1}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda\sum_{\frac{1}{C\lambda}\leq 2^{j}\leq\frac{C}{\lambda}}\left\|\nabla S_{j-1}u\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|\Delta_{j}\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\sum_{\frac{1}{C\lambda}\leq 2^{j}\leq\frac{C}{\lambda}}2^{j}\left\|\Delta_{j}v\right\|_{L^{2}}.

Using now the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality for finite sums, the characterization of
L^{2}
given in Proposition
C.2
and
\left(\sum_{\frac{1}{C\lambda}\leq 2^{j}\leq\frac{C}{\lambda}}2^{pj}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim\lambda^{-1}
we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|A_{1}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|v\right\|_{L^{2}}.

(C.16)

For
A_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\left[\Delta_{j}u,\Pi_{\leq}\right]S_{j-1}\nabla v

\displaystyle=\Delta_{j}u\Pi_{\leq}\left(S_{j-1}\nabla v\right)-\Pi_{\leq}\left(\Delta_{j}uS_{j-1}\nabla v\right).

(C.17)

If
3\times 2^{j}\leq 2^{N-1}
then
\Pi_{\leq}S_{j-1}=S_{j-1}
and (
C.10
) still implies
\Pi_{\leq}\left(\Delta_{j}uS_{j-1}\nabla v\right)=\Delta_{j}uS_{j-1}\nabla v
, so the two terms in (
C.17
) are equal. If
2^{j}\times\frac{1}{12}\geq 2^{N+1}
, then
\Pi_{\leq}S_{j-1}=\Pi_{\leq}
and
\Pi_{\leq}\left(\Delta_{j}uS_{j-1}\nabla v\right)=0
. Therefore there exists
C
independent from
\lambda
such that

\displaystyle\left\|A_{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\frac{1}{C\lambda}\leq 2^{j}\leq\frac{C}{\lambda}}\left\|\left[\Delta_{j}u,\Pi_{\leq}\right]S_{j-1}\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}+\sum_{\frac{C}{\lambda}\leq 2^{j}}\left\|\Delta_{j}u\Pi_{\leq}\left(\nabla v\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}.

We treat the first sum as we treated
A_{1}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{\frac{1}{C\lambda}\leq 2^{j}\leq\frac{C}{\lambda}}\left\|\left[\Delta_{j}u,\Pi_{\leq}\right]S_{j-1}\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda\sum_{\frac{1}{C\lambda}\leq 2^{j}\leq\frac{C}{\lambda}}\left\|\nabla\Delta_{j}u\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|S_{j-1}\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|v\right\|_{L^{2}}\lambda\sum_{\frac{1}{C\lambda}\leq 2^{j}\leq\frac{C}{\lambda}}2^{j}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|v\right\|_{L^{2}}.

For the second sum, we use (
C.12
) and then (
C.6
)

\displaystyle\sum_{\frac{C}{\lambda}\leq 2^{j}}\left\|\Delta_{j}u\Pi_{\leq}\left(\nabla v\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda}\left\|v\right\|_{L^{2}}\sum_{\frac{C}{\lambda}\leq 2^{j}}\left\|\Delta_{j}u\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda}\left\|v\right\|_{L^{2}}\sum_{\frac{C}{\lambda}\leq 2^{j}}2^{-2j}2^{\frac{7}{2}j}\left\|\Delta_{j}u\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda\left\|u\right\|_{H^{\frac{7}{2}}}\left\|v\right\|_{L^{2}},

where we used the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in
\ell^{2}
, the characterization of
H^{\frac{7}{2}}
given in Proposition
C.2
and
\left(\sum_{\frac{C}{\lambda}\leq 2^{j}}2^{-2j}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\lambda^{2}
. We finally obtain

\displaystyle\left\|A_{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\left(\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\lambda\left\|u\right\|_{H^{\frac{7}{2}}}\right)\left\|v\right\|_{L^{2}}.

(C.18)

For
A_{3}
, we define

\displaystyle R_{j}=\sum_{i=j-1}^{j+1}\left[\Delta_{i}u,\Pi_{\leq}\right]\Delta_{j}\nabla v=\sum_{i=j-1}^{j+1}\left(\Delta_{i}u\Pi_{\leq}\left(\Delta_{j}\nabla v\right)-\Pi_{\leq}\left(\Delta_{i}u\Delta_{j}\nabla v\right)\right),

(C.19)

so that
A_{3}=\sum_{j}R_{j}
.
If
2^{j}\times\frac{32}{3}\leq 2^{N-1}
then (
C.11
) implies
\Pi_{\leq}\left(\Delta_{i}u\Delta_{j}\nabla v\right)=\Delta_{i}u\Delta_{j}\nabla v
and (
C.4
) implies
\Pi_{\leq}\Delta_{j}=\Delta_{j}
, so that the two terms in (
C.19
) are equal and
R_{j}=0
. Therefore there exists a constant
C>0
independent from
\lambda
such that

\displaystyle\left\|A_{3}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq\sum_{\frac{C}{\lambda}\leq 2^{j}}\sum_{i=j-1}^{j+1}\left\|\left[\Delta_{i}u,\Pi_{\leq}\right]\Delta_{j}\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}.

We use (
C.14
), (
C.5
) and (
C.6
)

\displaystyle\left\|A_{3}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda\sum_{\frac{C}{\lambda}\leq 2^{j}}\sum_{i=j-1}^{j+1}2^{\frac{5}{2}i+j}\left\|\Delta_{i}u\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\Delta_{j}v\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda\sum_{\frac{C}{\lambda}\leq 2^{j}}2^{\frac{7}{2}j}\left\|\Delta_{j}u\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\Delta_{j}v\right\|_{L^{2}},

where we neglect the sum over
i
. Using again the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in
\ell^{2}
, the characterization of
H^{\frac{7}{2}}
and
L^{2}
given in Proposition
C.2
we finally obtain

\displaystyle\left\|A_{3}\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lambda\left\|u\right\|_{H^{\frac{7}{2}}}\left\|v\right\|_{L^{2}}.

Together with (
C.16
) and (
C.18
) this last estimate concludes the proof of Lemma
3.2
.
